(B).

To this end, we first perform a weighted blow-up

\text{WBU}(n,0,-1,\frac{m}{n},\frac{k_{1}}{n},\bullet)
at the
singular point of order
n
on the maximal orbi-sphere, as in
Proposition
4.5
-(IV). This generates an exceptional divisor
that is an
S^{1}
-invariant orbi-sphere with north pole a singular
point of order
m
on the maximal orbi-surface and south pole a
singular point of order
k_{1}
(see the labeled multigraph on the right
in the first row of Figure
5.4
). By
Corollary
4.11
and (
3.13
), the normal
orbi-bundle of the maximal orbi-sphere
\Sigma
in the blown-up
Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space has degree

\frac{q}{sn}-\frac{k_{1}}{nm}=x-\frac{p}{ms}.

Then we perform
r-1
weighted blow-ups
\text{WBU}(1,0,-1,1,k_{i},\bullet)
at
r-1
regular points in
\Sigma
,
and obtain a space with
r
interior fixed points of orders

k_{1},\ldots,k_{r}
(see the labeled multigraph on the left in the
second row of Figure
5.4
). By
Corollary
4.11
, this reduces the degree of the
normal orbi-bundle of the maximal orbi-sphere to

-\frac{p}{ms}
. Since this degree is negative we can blow-down the
maximal orbi-sphere and obtain a singular point of order
p
and the
multigraph (B) of Theorem
5.8
. Note that we can
choose the appropriate blow-up sizes so that the moment map labels
satisfy condition
(vi)
.

\bullet

Multigraph
(C)
:
For positive integers
m_{1},m_{2},p,c
such that
m_{1},m_{2},p
are pairwise coprime,
consider the quotient

M:=\mathbb{C}P^{2}(m_{1},m_{2},p)/\mathbb{Z}_{c}(l,1,0)
as in
Example
1.96
, with an
S^{1}
-action given by

\displaystyle\phi:S^{1}\times M

\displaystyle\to M

\displaystyle(\lambda,[z_{0}:z_{1}:z_{2}]_{c})

\displaystyle\mapsto[z_{0}:z_{1}:\lambda z_{2}]_{c}=[\lambda^{-\frac{m_{1}}{p}}z_{0}:\lambda^{-\frac{m_{2}}{p}}z_{1}:z_{2}],

We endow
M
with the symplectic form of
Example
1.105
. The above action is
Hamiltonian and the labeled multigraph for the resulting Hamiltonian

S^{1}
-space
(M,\omega,H)
is depicted on the left of the first row of
Figure
5.5
. We claim that a Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space with labeled multigraph as in Theorem
5.8
(C) can be
obtained from
(M,\omega,H)
by performing finitely many

S^{1}
-equivariant weighted blow-ups and
blow-downs (see Figure
5.5
). As above, we do not specify
the size of each blow-up.

m_{1}c
m_{2}c
{\frac{1}{m_{1}c}(1,l_{1}),\frac{1}{m_{2}c}(1,l_{2})}
{g=0,A}
{\frac{1}{pc}(1,l_{max})}
…
m_{1}c
m_{2}c
{\frac{1}{m_{1}c}(1,l_{1}),\frac{1}{m_{2}c}(1,l_{2})}

{\frac{1}{k_{1}}(1,k_{1}-1)}
{\frac{1}{k_{r}}(1,k_{r}-1)}

m_{1}c

{\frac{1}{c^{2}m_{1}m_{2}(k_{1}+\dots+k_{n})-pc}(1,l_{min})}
{\frac{1}{pc}(1,l_{max})}

{\frac{1}{k_{r}}(1,k_{r}-1)}

Figure 5.5.
Construction of a Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space with Multigraph
(C)
.

The space
(M,\omega,H)
has a fixed orbi-sphere

\Sigma_{-}\simeq\mathbb{C}P^{1}(m_{1},m_{2})/\mathbb{Z}_{c}
with normal
orbi-bundle that has degree
\beta=\frac{p}{cm_{1}m_{2}}
(see
(
3.13
)). We consider integers
k_{1},\ldots,k_{r}\geq 1

such that
m_{1}m_{2}\,c\,(k_{1}+\cdots+k_{r})>p
and perform
r
weighted
blow-ups,
\text{WBU}(1,1,0,k_{i},1,\bullet)
of
X
at
r
regular
points in
\Sigma_{-}
(see the labeled multigraph on the right of the
first row of Figure
5.5
). We obtain
r
non-extremal fixed points with cyclic orbifold structure
groups of orders
k_{1},\ldots,k_{r}
. By Lemma
4.11

the degree of the normal orbi-bundle of the blown-up minimal orbi-sphere
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{-}
becomes

\widetilde{\beta}=\frac{p}{cm_{1}m_{2}}-k_{1}-\cdots-k_{r}<0.

Hence we can blow-down
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{-}
, using the reverse
procedure of a weighted blow-up

\text{WBU}(qc,\frac{m_{1}}{q},\frac{m_{2}}{q},\frac{m_{1}}{q},\frac{m_{2}}{q},\bullet)
,
obtaining a singular point of order

qc:=c^{2}m_{1}m_{2}(k_{1}+\dots+k_{n})-pc
. This yields a Hamiltonian

S^{1}
-space with labeled multigraph as in the bottom of Figure

5.5
, i.e., as in Theorem
5.8

(C). It can be checked that the labels satisfy condition
(iv)
. Moreover, we can choose the appropriate
blow-up sizes so that the moment map labels satisfy condition

(v)
.

Remark 5.9
.

At present, we do not know whether there is an even more explicit
description of a general minimal Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space with
labeled multigraph as in Theorem
5.8
(B) with

r>1
, or (C). In some specific cases, we know the answer: For
instance, the example studied in
[
50
]
is a global
quotient of
\mathbb{C}P^{1}\times\mathbb{C}P^{1}
and has labeled multigraph of
type (C) – see Example
3.13
.

Lemma
5.7
,
Theorems
5.1
and
5.8
, and
the above constructions immediately imply the following
result.

Theorem 5.10
.

Any Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space with only isolated fixed points can be obtained by applying finitely many

S^{1}
-equivariant symplectic weighted blow-ups to either

(A)

a quotient of
\mathbb{C}P^{1}\times\mathbb{C}P^{1}
by a finite
cyclic group;

(B)

a quotient of a weighted projective plane by a finite
cyclic group or a weighted blow-up of one of these spaces at a
singular point and at a certain number of regular points on the
same orbi-sphere, followed by a weighted blow-down of this
orbi-sphere; or

(C)

a weighted blow-up of a quotient of a weighted
projective plane at a certain number of regular points on an
orbi-sphere followed by an equivariant weighted blow-down of this
orbi-sphere.

To conclude this section, we point out an important difference between
Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-manifolds and Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-manifolds: while in
the former, the
S^{1}
-action of any minimal spaces with only isolated
fixed points can be extended to a toric one (cf.
[
21
, Theorem
5.1]
), this no longer holds in the latter. More specifically,
the following result holds.

Lemma 5.11
.

The
S^{1}
-action on a minimal Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space with labeled
multigraph as in Theorem
5.8
(C) does not extend
to a toric action.

Proof.

Let
(M,\omega,H)
be as in the statement. Since
m_{1}c,m_{2}c>1
and
since there is at least one non-extremal isolated fixed point, there
is a level set of
H
that contains at least three orbits with
non-trivial stabilizer. By the classification of symplectic toric
orbifolds in
[
29
]
, this cannot hold if the

S^{1}
-action can be extended to a toric one.
∎

Remark 5.12
.

There is an alternative proof of Lemma
5.11

that does not use
[
29
]
. Let
(M,\omega,H)
be as in
the statement of the lemma and suppose that the
S^{1}
-action extends
to a toric one. The desingularization procedure of Theorem

4.12
works
mutatis mutandis
for toric
actions. Hence, we may apply finitely many toric weighted blow-ups
to obtain a symplectic toric manifold. Moreover, we may choose the
sizes of the blow-up so that the symplectic toric manifold has the
property that there is a level set of
H
contains three orbits with
non-trivial stabilizer. This is because the corresponding property
holds for the original space
(M,\omega,H)
. This contradicts

[
21
, Proposition 5.21]
.

6.
Final remarks

6.1.
Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-spaces are Kähler

Theorem 6.1
.

Every Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space is Kähler, i.e., it admits a
complex structure for which the symplectic form is Kähler and
the
S^{1}
-action is holomorphic.

Proof.

Let
(M,\omega,H)
be a Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space. By Theorem

4.12
, it can be desingularized after applying finitely many

S^{1}
-equivariant weighted blow-ups. Let
k
be the number of
blow-ups needed in the desingularization procedure given by the
proof of Theorem
4.12
. We argue by induction
on
k
. If
k=0
, the result follows by
[
21
, Theorem
7.1]
. Suppose that the result holds for
k-1
. After
applying the first blow-up in the desingularization procedure, we
obtain a Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space
(M^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},H^{\prime})
that requires

k-1
blow-ups to be desingularized. Hence, by the inductive
hypothesis,
(M^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},H^{\prime})
is Kähler. Since
(M,\omega,H)
is
(isomorphic to a space) obtained by performing a weighted blow-up on

(M^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},H^{\prime})
, the result follows by Remark
4.15
.
∎

6.2.
Birational equivalence of minimal Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-spaces

The aim of this section is to prove that some of the minimal Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-spaces obtained in Sections
5.2.3
and
5.2.5
are
S^{1}
-equivariantly birationally equivalent.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
(M,\omega,H)
be a minimal Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space that has either fixed orbi-spheres or no
fixed orbi-surfaces. Then

(M,\omega,H)
is birationally equivalent to a minimal
Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space with multigraph as in
(B)
of
Theorem
5.8
.

Proof.

We assume first that any fixed orbi-surface of
(M,\omega,H)
has
genus zero. By Section
5.2.5
, the result holds if the labeled multigraph
of
(M,\omega,H)
is either as in (I) or (II) in Theorem

5.5
(see Figure

5.4
). Hence, we may assume that the labeled
multigraph is as in (III) in Theorem

5.5
with
g=0
. Let
\Sigma_{\pm}
denote
the orbi-sphere lying at the maximum/minimum of
H
and let
\beta_{\pm}
be the degree of the normal
orbi-bundle of
\Sigma_{\pm}
. Since there are no interior fixed
points in
M
, it follows that
\beta_{+}+\beta_{-}=0
(cf. equation
(
3.11
)). Without loss of generality, we assume that
\beta_{-}\leq 0
. We fix notation so that the labeled multigraph of
(M,\omega,H)
is
as in the leftmost in Figure
6.1
, where
n
is the
number of singular points on
\Sigma_{\pm}
and we allow
n=0
. The Seifert invariant of the circle orbi-bundle
associated to the normal orbi-bundle of
\Sigma_{-}
is

(g=0,\beta_{0},(p_{1},\hat{l}_{1}^{\prime}),\cdots,(p_{n},\hat{l}_{n}^{\prime})),

where, as usual,
1\leq\hat{l}_{i}^{\prime}<p_{i}
is such that

\hat{l}_{i}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{l}_{i}=1\mod p_{i}
.

If
n>0
, for each

i=1,\ldots,n
, we perform a weighted blow up

\text{WBU}(p_{i},1,0,\frac{\hat{l}_{i}^{\prime}}{p_{i}},\frac{1}{p_{i}},\varepsilon_{i})
at each of the singular points in

\Sigma_{-}
, where
\varepsilon_{i}>0
is sufficiently
small. As a
result, for each
i
, after the blow-up we obtain an interior
singular point of type
\frac{1}{\hat{l}_{i}^{\prime}}(1,\hat{s}_{i})
,
where
1\leq\hat{s}_{i}<\hat{l}_{1}^{\prime}
is such that
\hat{s}_{i}\,p_{i}=-1\mod\hat{l}_{i}^{\prime}
(see the second multigraph from the
left in Figure
6.1
).

By Corollary
4.11
and
Proposition
1.66
, the degree of the normal
orbi-bundle of the resulting minimal sphere is
\beta_{-}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\hat{l}_{i}^{\prime}}{p_{i}}=\beta_{0}<0
and so we can
blow down this sphere, obtaining a singular point of order
\lvert\beta_{0}\rvert
. For this we use the reverse construction of the
weighted blow up

\text{WBU}\left(\beta_{0},\frac{1}{\beta_{0}},\frac{1}{\beta_{0}},\frac{1}{\beta_{0}},\frac{1}{\beta_{0}},\varepsilon\right)
for an appropriate
\varepsilon>0
. We obtain the third multigraph in Figure
6.1
. By
Lemma
5.4
and Theorem
5.5
, by applying finitely many

S^{1}
-equivariant weighted blow-downs, we obtain a minimal Hamiltonian

S^{1}
-space with no interior fixed points that has precisely one fixed
orbi-sphere at the maximum, i.e., with labeled multigraph given by (I) in
Theorem
5.5
(see the rightmost labeled
graph in Figure
6.1
). Hence, the result follows in
this case. If
n=0
, we argue as above starting with a standard blow-up
to the smooth sphere
\Sigma_{-}
.

Next we assume that
(M,\omega,H)
only has isolated fixed points. If
the multigraph is as in
(A)
of Theorem
5.8
, we
can, for instance, blow up the singular point of type

\frac{1}{c}(1,l)
, using a

\text{WBU}(c,\frac{m}{c},-\frac{n}{c},\frac{1}{c},\frac{l}{c},\bullet)
,
resulting in an exceptional divisor with one singular point of order

l
(see the second multigraph in Figure
6.2
). By
Corollary
4.11
, the normal orbi-bundles of the
blown-up
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
-isotropy orbi-spheres in the resulting
space have degrees
-\frac{l}{c}
and
-\frac{1}{lc}

respectively. Blowing down these orbi-spheres we obtain the
Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space with the last multigraph in
Figure
6.2
(we leave the details to the reader).

Example 6.3
.

Let us consider a minimal Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space with
multigraph as in Theorem
5.5
-(III), with

g=0
and such that each fixed orbi-sphere has exactly two singular
points (see Figure
6.3
). Since there are no interior
fixed points, the degree
\beta_{\pm}
of the normal orbi-bundle of
the fixed orbi-sphere
\Sigma_{\pm}
is zero. Moreover, by
(iv)
and

(v)
of Theorem
5.5
-(III), if the type
of one of the singular points in
\Sigma_{+}
is
\frac{1}{p}(1,l)
,
then the type of the other singular point in
\Sigma_{+}
must be

\frac{1}{p}(1,p-l)
and the multigraph is the one in
Figure
6.3
, where
1\leq l^{\prime}<p
is such that
l\cdot l^{\prime}=1\mod p
.

\frac{1}{p}(1,p-l^{\prime})\,\,\,\,\frac{1}{p}(1,l^{\prime})
g=0,A
\frac{1}{p}(1,l)\quad\,\,\frac{1}{p}(1,p-l)
g=0,A
p
p

Figure 6.3.
Multigraph of a minimal Hamiltonian
S^{1}
-space with two orbi-spheres with trivial normal orbi-bundle.

Since the Seifert invariant of the circle orbi-bundle associated to the normal orbi-bundle of
\Sigma_{-}
is

(g=0,\beta_{0}=-1,(p,l^{\prime}),(p,p-l^{\prime})),

the proof of Theorem
6.2
shows that this space
is
S^{1}
-equivariantly birationally equivalent to
S^{2}\times S^{2}
with the standard diagonal circle action.
